Architect's bridge from this suite to the next phase. One entry per gap; factual; evidence is
a `blade emit`/`blade plan` excerpt, a diagnostic code, or a `src/` location found cheaply.
"Direction" is one line, not a plan. Lane column: whether the C++ and LLVM lanes differ.
Evidence dated 2026-10-17, compiler 0.20.0, this worktree's `bin/Release/net10.0/Blade.exe`;
blade-B's items cite `notes/llvm-acceptance-B.md`.

| G6 | **FIXED (agent D, merge b3272712: M1 retained-block pool cache in BOTH allocators + M3 let-rec zero-fill elision via a new `IRUninit` leaf, formalism §7.5; M0 shim build identity).** D's scratch A/B (full size, serial, 7 interleaved runs, medians; NOT harness-measured): recur C++ 136.4 → 23.8 ms, LLVM 131.5 → 22.3 ms; page faults 106,906 → 13,626 and 94,606 → 13,277; checksum identical. Still open on this kernel: 4.5b(ii) destination passing and 4.5c stage fusion (G8). Historical entry: recur (serial) — **pilot-diagnosed: the ALLOCATOR, not the arithmetic**; phase-2 plan adds two unrecorded passes (plan §1.5, §4.5): a 48 MB ZERO-FILL of the whole trajectory before the recursion (`src/TypeCheckInfer.fs:13840`) and a 48 MB COPY of each step's result into the trajectory row — fixes 4.5b(i) zero-fill elision and 4.5b(ii) destination passing, after the retained-block cache 4.5a | Scalar RK4 stages in one `do x` loop, no stage arrays | Each time step allocates and frees 8 temporaries of 160 KB (4 `rhs` results, 3 stage inputs, the step result) in both lanes; the Windows heap returns freed blocks of that size as fresh pages, so each allocation re-takes ~38 page faults at ~1.3 µs: 300 steps x 8 x 38 ≈ 91k faults ≈ 118 ms — the measured 19.6 ns per cell-step against ~2.5 ns of arithmetic (pilot: 118 ms vs the expert's 10–11 ms, an 11x loss). The stage loops themselves are fully vectorized AVX2, no bounds checks, direct row pointers, no per-cell calls; `blade plan` shows pool reuse DECLINED in `step`. SCHEDULING ADMISSION MATRIX -- every pass asks `Optimize.scheduleMayWrite` (W: the shared provenance-aware `writeAnalyzer` over the module's lets) about all five parties; `cseFactsOf` (F: MayWrite, R: Repeatable) stands beside it where it already did: hot-row RowFold = producer W+F, consumer W+R, outer kernel W+F, seed W+F, between W+R; cell epilogue (ChunkFold) = producer W, consumer W (+ the Identity/Square cell-term whitelist), outer W, seed W, between W+R; compact row classes = producer W+F, consumer (walk) W+F, outer W+F, seed W+F, between W+F; row-fold fusion = producer (the recursion, its own buffer local) W + bufferUseOk/callsRepeatable, consumer W + tilePureBody, outer W + tilePureBody, seed W, between (the seed statements, inside the producer block) W + bufferUseOk/callsRepeatable; compaction fusion = producer the stored compound source, consumer the mask predicate (predOk: pure, total), outer builtin `+` only, seed none (unseeded), between W+F. The matrix covers the PRODUCER-SCHEDULING passes only (it is not a claim that every reordering pass uses one predicate): domain diagonal walks keep domainFoldOrder / domainMapKernelPure, invariant hoisting and CSE their cseFactsOf / deferred-name checks, sibling-fold sharing its own writesIn closure, elementwise / slice-write fusion tilePureBody, G35 row slots the IRTotality whitelist, G7 joins joinEffectAudit + joinThreadAudit, W2-A scatter its strict pure-body recognition over stored sources. The planners resolve callees through the PROGRAM's functions (`scheduleMayWriteIn programFuncs`), so an imported pure producer no longer declines. The backend analysis's former conservatism (CopiedLet = false in CodeGenExprSupport) is gone with G46: it now reads Lowering's rule (`isCopiedArrayLet`), since every lane copies. that shape was G46's false alias, and the case is now `omp_nest_kernel_let_mut_copy_keeps_team`); 21 the parallel-body refusal keeps the function's OWN `mut` parameters apart from captures (`loops/502` runs; the 843cb654 refusal of it was a regression); and a self-recursive function is summarized as the LEAST fixpoint from "writes nothing" (the pessimistic seed made a pure recursive helper forwarding an array look like a writer, so 843cb654 refused `examples/mandelbrot.bladenb`'s `where omp` `far_grid` -- found by a 181-file rescan; `loops/504` pins the shape); Codex review W2F-6: 22 a callback JOINS every candidate any call site passes, at each application (the resolver used to pick one candidate on a boolean: `launch(forward)` before `launch(bump)` compiled; `mutability-errors/030`, reversed order `031`, a pure candidate forwarded through recursion keeps its team `loops/505`); 23 recursion is a true fixpoint whose budget (64 rounds) widens to TOP (External plus every storage parameter) and drops the summaries built on the unfinished value (a ten-parameter rotation reaching a captured array compiled before: `032`; a callback through a recursive helper `033`); Codex review W2F-7: 24 a NULLARY function type (the empty-slot arrow `() -> T`) is a function for the callback table (the value-slot filter of W2F-6 skipped it, so a pure `launch(one)` was refused BL4005: `loops/506` runs and keeps its team, the writing twin `mutability-errors/035`); 25 the callback table is ONE collector, `Optimize.callbackTable`, shared by Lowering's parallel-body refusal and both emitters, computed to a FIXPOINT over indirect calls -- `h(f)` with `h` a callback parameter is resolved to ALL of h's candidates and propagates its actuals into each target's parameter slots (`via(launch, bump)` used to leave `bump` out of launch's set: `mutability-errors/036`, reversed order `037`, a writing lambda argument `038`; all-pure `loops/507` and pure-lambda `loops/508` keep the team) -- and a callback parameter of a function that ESCAPES untracked (referenced other than as a call head or argument, or passed to an unresolvable call) is TAINTED: no table entry, so its applications read as unknown (a write); (b) a 68-parameter rotation reaching a captured array is still climbing at round 64 and is refused through the TOP widening (`039`). Codex review W2F-8 replaced that collector's matching with a monotone abstract interpretation solved to its LEAST fixpoint (`Optimize.callbackTable`): every tracked name -- callback parameter, local `let`, MODULE binding (its identity kept) -- starts at BOTTOM and grows by joins; a call feeds every target its head may denote, known targets kept beside an unknown one; TOP comes only from an untracked name, a function a call returns, or an ESCAPE (returned, stored, used as a kernel, handed to an unknown call or a non-callback parameter); tuples are followed component-wise; a reassigned tracked name joins the new value; over budget the table widens to empty (every callback unknown). 26 a module alias `let run = launch` is an alias, not an escape (804516d1 refused the pure `loops/509`); 27 a local alias of a callback, called directly or through a tuple, is evaluated, not matched by name (804516d1 compiled the writers `mutability-errors/040`, `041`; pure controls `loops/510`, `511` keep the team); 28 an undiscovered callback is bottom, not unknown, so the answer is independent of discovery order (804516d1 refused the pure third-order `loops/512`; reversed order `513`). Deliberately conservative: the join-leg refusal and the planners' `scheduleMayWrite` / `scheduleMayWriteIn` install `Callbacks = None`, so ANY callback application there reads as a write (a declined schedule / BL4005 on a join leg, never a wrong answer). Not expressible today, so not pinned: candidates writing different `mut` parameter positions (a function type has no `mut` parameters). Own-parameter writes inside a `where omp` KERNEL are refused by the front end (the parameter is bound outside the kernel body) for both the same-cell race (`034`) and the per-cell-disjoint variant -- a conservatism; a function's own non-kernel body may write it (`loops/502`); `blade test omp-pragma` (decline needles + provenance positives), `omp-reduce` (`g41_*` runtime matrix: serial build, T = 1, 5x T = 8, total AND counter), `llvm goldens` (no fold FMF over a writing map) | both (C++: teams, K lanes, simd; LLVM: fold fast-math over a writing map -- it opens no team) | done |

CONTRACT DECIDED (formalism 10.2, new paragraph "Effectful named computations"): a `let` bound to a bare map whose evaluation WRITES (kernel, callee, or named deferred producer it reads; the shared `scheduleMayWriteIn` predicate) is evaluated ONCE, COMPLETELY, at its definition, in ascending storage order -- as `\|> compute` would -- in every scope; a pure map keeps the deferred reading. "Evaluate per read everywhere" was rejected: a function body has no downstream forcing site (S2's reason to exist), so per-read would need new codegen. Implemented as `Lowering.forceEffectfulModuleLets` (module bindings and top-level block spines, before `refuseWritingJoinLegs`) plus `TypeCheckInfer.tryInferReduceCompute`'s `namedWritingProducer` decline (the fold splice copied a NAMED producer into each fold, so forcing the let alone gave THREE evaluations, 68 / counter 12). S2's join exemption is kept in both scopes: a writing map read ONLY as reduction-join operands stays deferred and is refused (BL4005, `mutability-errors/014`, `020`; the C++ reduction-join emitter needs the leg unforced); a writing map with any other reader (alias, view, tuple, fold) is forced and a join through that reader reads stored cells -- which is what a function body already did, so five former G43 refusals are now ACCEPTANCES with one value in every lane (`mutability-errors/015, 018, 024, 025, 026` moved to `loops/519`-`523`). Deferred WRAPPERS (choice / fallback / guard / sequence) are not forced; a join over a writing one stays refused (`mutability-errors/023`). Contract (formalism 2.7, new paragraph): an assignable array `let` of an existing array holds a deep copy with the source's storage class in every scope and lane, compound / sparse / ragged / dependent-index stores excepted (the interpreter's `isDenseCopyableArray`, genVarAliasBinding's guard). `CodeGenExpr.kernelLetCopy` makes the expression-position and statement-position kernel-body lets copy (`materializeArrayCopyForm`) whenever the module records the let as assignable (`IRModule.MutableArrayLets`, now installed for the emitters as `programMutableArrayLetsCell`); the copy is freed at the IIFE's end when it provably dies there (scalar result, only full element reads / writes / extents), else left to the IIFE leak convention; it is ELIDED when unobservable (no assignment, call or callable reference in scope and a storage-free result), so a read-only `let row = X` in a kernel allocates nothing. The backend write facts now read such a let as a private copy (`CopiedLet = isCopiedArrayLet`, Lowering's rule), so a `where omp` kernel writing its copy keeps the team (`deallocArgsFor` moved to CodeGenState to free the copy) | corpus `loops/517` (write through the copy, the same under `where omp`, the copy handed to a `mut` parameter, a plain `let c = Gm` read after the kernel writes Gm, a read-only alias, a SYMMETRIC initializer; all lanes); omp-pragma `omp_nest_kernel_let_mut_copy_keeps_team` (replaces `omp_nest_write_veto_census`: pragma + `std::copy_n`, no census -- the nest veto has no other accepted shape now, every other parallel write to captured storage is BL4005 in Lowering) | C++ lane | done |

Parameters that may hold a function -- a function type or a tuple with a function leaf -- are tracked, tuples component-wise; only function-typed names are exported. Now a fold operand naming a deferred wrapper (`CodeGenFusion.isDeferredWrapperOperand`, through deferred aliases) is forced first, like a positional read: `collectDeferredPositionalReads` notes it under IRReduce (expression position: blocks, `reduce(q) + reduce(q)`), and genBindingDispatch's IRReduce arm forces it (`forceDeferredArrayInput`) before genReduceBinding. In MAIN the nodes only MARK themselves: a top-level BLOCK statement (a let chain) FORCES UP FRONT, before any block effect or alias upgrade, the C++ lane's pre-pass set in its order (fourth fix round after codex-diff-W4B-B-4: EmitLlvm.collectPositionalReadsLl, the SAME walk shape as CodeGenFusion.collectDeferredPositionalReads line for line -- IRIndex / IRExtent / IRContains bases, assignment RHSs, call arguments, every prodsum operand, a fold over a deferred choice / fallback; the slice / curry / subset children only; a chain ending in a tuple, its lets' values only, genLetChainBinding's rule -- over the lane's live deferred state, Ctx.StmtForceOrder), EVERY collected map with no exception (fifth fix round after codex-diff-W4B-B-5 finding 1: the fourth round skipped a map whose KERNEL audited total, but its deferred INPUT could fail -- `bad = map(x, 10 / v); h = map(bad, v + 1); later = map(x, x((v + 5 : Row)))`, `{ let first = h(0); first + h(1) + later(0) + later(1) }` over x = [1, 0] reached later's BL8006 at line 5 in LLVM alone; now bad's BL8013 at line 3, as in C++ and the base; producerCannotFail, still used by the partial / any-order retention, now also requires every input array to be stored or a range); any other ordinary force keeps the BASE boundary, the START of the whole statement. Fix for all three: follow aggregate carriers and projections by binder identity (a), store the first forced value with dominating lifetime or refuse the shape (b), force / copy at a local alias in every scope (c); pin each.** **FIXED (round 3 wave 4, W4-1, fix round after reviews/wave4-codex-reviews.md#codex-diff-w4-1 findings 2, 3, 6; the alias / forcing contract, formalism 10.2 "Aliases and printing of deferred maps").** `let g = method_for(x) <@> ...; let g2 = g` printed `g` (never `g2`) in the C++ lane, `g2` in the interpreter, both in the LLVM lane; the C++ lane forced `g` AT the alias | -- | CONTRACT: a top-level alias of a deferred map is the SAME deferred value: it evaluates nothing; a use through it is that use of the source (a fold fuses: the type checker resolves a TOP-LEVEL alias to a fixpoint, stopping at a block-local binder, TypeCheckInfer.blockLocalVids, by identity); a deferred map and its top-level aliases form ONE ALIAS CLASS (`IRLift.deferredAliasClasses`, shared by the three lanes' print passes) that prints under every name iff any member was forced at module level (C++ genPrintStatements declares an unforced member as a reference to a forced one; the interpreter's Run.fs reads the forced member's cells; the LLVM lane marks the class); a WRITE through a deferred binder -- element, whole reassignment, a `let` view, a writing `mut` argument (Optimize.callableWriteSummaryWith) -- is REFUSED at lowering (`Lowering.refuseDeferredWritesAndUnorderedIfs`, BL4005, by binder identity: module bindings, their aliases, bare maps in top-level blocks), so no member of a class can diverge from another and no fused fold can follow a write. A block / function-body alias is a local copy made at the alias (the C++ lane and the interpreter's IRLet arm), ordinary storage. FIX ROUND 2 (second look findings 1-3): the written-binder judgment is a PROGRAM-WIDE FIXPOINT by binder identity (`Lowering.refuseDeferredWritesAndUnorderedIfs`): the binders a write's target may belong to -- through every `let` view, alias, REBINDING and `if` of one, in any scope, stopping only at a PROVED copy (an assignable `let a = Z` of a plain dense array) or fresh storage; a walk that exhausts its budget REFUSES -- plus every argument a call passes to a parameter in the set (any number of hops), every array argument of an UNRESOLVED callee (a callback: conservative, even a reading one is refused), and the inputs of a map whose kernel writes a parameter. It replaces the first fix round's use of Optimize.callableWriteSummaryWith, whose walk stops at the first external write. The LLVM lane canonicalizes an alias class at the FORCING EVENT (materializeExpr: every slot holding the forced producer object is upgraded to the forced pool), so a read or print of any alias reads the stored cells and never reruns the producer over inputs written since; the class value is fixed at its first forcing event in every lane. FIX ROUND: (finding 2) the LLVM lane's reassignment no longer needs a forcing order -- refused; (finding 3) forcing the source now prints every alias (class rule), and writes in either direction are refused; (finding 6) the by-NAME write veto in the fused fold is gone (TypeCheck.moduleAssignedNames reverted): shadowing writers no longer change the module fold, pinned. ## Notes

- G1 is the one gap that turns a hypothesised loss into a win on the suite's headline kernel
  (its mechanism was corrected by blade-A: a single-accumulator dot, not a jam); G15 is the
  C++-lane-only trap that decided the `prodsum` spelling; G16 is a correctness finding, not a
  performance gap, and lives here only so the next phase does not lose it;
  G2/G3 are the same missing primitive seen from two sides; G4 removed a kernel from the
  suite; G7/G11 are mt8 holes; G8 contradicts the idiom ladder's top rung; G12/G13 are
  contract-shaping constraints the Blade sources carry.
- Items marked "blade-B" are taken from `notes/llvm-acceptance-B.md`; the architect did not
  re-run them. Items with a probe file name were emitted by the architect at extents 11–1009
  (no timing).

- Round 3 W1-3 (deferred-materialization defects in the C++ lane; branch
  `worktree-agent-a140e832f4c815aa6` off the wave-3 tip 66e04875: 142ee9d7 plus the Codex-review
  fix commit; correctness only, no performance claim). Four BL9002s (g++ rejected the emitted
  C++ while the interpreter ran the program), each its own corpus pin -- every one failed on
  66e04875 and runs on the fix with the interpreter's values -- plus a control that compiled
  before and after:
  (1) D-B1 `compound(<unforced inline map>, mask(...))`: `genCompoundInitBinding`'s computed-
  operand arm emitted the operand through genBinding, which DEFERS a map (only
  `// matched__dense = <deferred computation>`), and discarded the context, so the scatter read
  an undeclared `matched__dense`. Now `materializeCompoundOperand` threads the context and forces
  the temporary like a let-bound deferred operand -- on BOTH operands: the mask arm had the same
  hole (an inline Bool map, `inline_map__masksrc`; a still-deferred named Bool map read by name,
  `'m' was not declared`). `sql-masks/014` (rank-2 equijoin pools + rank-1), control `015`
  (`|> compute` spelling), mask operand `016`; operand order `017` (both operands computed in
  place and failing: the dense operand's BL8006 before the mask's BL8003) with its valid-data
  control `018` (BL8003). `017` exposed the LLVM lane materializing the MASK first (BL8003):
  `EmitLlvm.emitCompoundInit` now materializes the dense operand first, the C++ lane's and the
  interpreter's order (`llvm goldens` unchanged).
  (2) D-B2 `unique` over a rank-1 compound spelled `.extents[0]` / `operator[]`, which
  `Compound` lacks; `intersect` / `union` had the same walk. One `setOpOperandWalk`
  (`.idx->cardinality` cells through `.data[i]`, the interpreter's compoundToDense /
  sparseToDense order and `sort`'s existing convention). `sql-unique-contains/009` (the
  semijoin + group shape), dense control `010`, `sql-set-ops/012`, and the SPARSE operand
  `sql-set-ops/013` (supplied-key order, with an empty-compound control).
  (3) Fixture 02's tuple of computes `(h |> compute, y |> compute)` returned from a block:
  genScalarExprBinding rendered each `IRCompute (IRVar deferred)` member by NAME (exprToCpp's
  IRCompute arm names a var) -- `std::make_tuple(__v42, __v49)` of two undeclared names. Now
  `genMemberOrderedTupleBinding` evaluates such a tuple's members LEFT TO RIGHT, each at its own
  position, dispatched BEFORE the binding's whole-expression positional-read pre-pass (which
  forced a later member's producer ahead of an earlier member's compute): an explicit compute
  is computed into a temporary (y's capture of h forces h under its own name on the way, as the
  interpreter does), and EVERY other evaluating member is bound to a temporary at its own
  position (C++17 leaves `std::make_tuple`'s argument order unspecified; g++ goes right to
  left). A let chain ending in a tuple leaves the tuple's reads to the final binding
  (genLetChainBinding). `tuples/034`, stored control `035`; first-failure order `037`, `038`
  (compute, then a deferred read: BL8013), `039` (reversed: BL8006), `040` (safe compute, then a
  cast and a division: the cast), `043` (the let-chain spelling: BL8013 -- this one compiled on
  66e04875 and reported the read's BL8006). Neither the block nor the compact pool is the
  cause: a top-level dense `(h |> compute, y |> compute)` failed identically.
  (4) RESULTS 15.4's card: its nested-kernel probe had been closed by 04b00fe8 (`loops/524`);
  of the card's other use sites (a diagonal read, a nested map walk, a deferred compact source,
  a block, a function body) only a TUPLE member still failed -- `std::make_tuple(g, 2.0)` for
  the explicit `(g |> compute, 2.0)` -- and it is the SAME site as (3): the base emission shows
  the identical by-name `std::make_tuple` render for both, and the one tuple arm fixes both with
  no other change reaching them. `tuples/036` (with the G45 writing control: stored once,
  counter 6, through two explicit computes of it and beside a pure computed member capturing
  it). A BARE deferred member `(g, 1.0)` is NOT closed: the interpreter holds it unforced inside
  the tuple (Interp/Core.fs IRTuple; forcing a tuple does not force its members) and evaluates
  it only when a later consumer forces it, so computing it at the tuple changes the first
  failure; the C++ lane refuses it at codegen with the existing message "unevaluated
  computation used as value - use |> compute" (BL7004; `tuples/041`), and `g |> compute` is the
  supported spelling (`tuples/042`: BL8013). The LLVM lane still computes it eagerly
  (pre-existing; a gap row at merge: the shared bare-member contract).
  Shared causes: (3) and (4)'s residual are one site; (1) is the same SYMPTOM (a by-name
  consumer of an unforced computation) at a different site with a different fix; (2) is an
  operand-access defect, unrelated. Lanes: the C++ lane, plus the LLVM lane's compound operand
  order in (1). In `blade test llvm` the LLVM lane REFUSES rank-2 compound inits, IRContains and
  IRIntersect / IRUnique, and sparse-index initializers, so `sql-masks/014` and `015`, `sql-unique-contains/009` and `010`, and
  `sql-set-ops/012` and `013` are SKIPS (the differential never hands a refused program to the
  C++ emitter); `sql-masks/016`-`018` and `tuples/034`-`040`, `042`, `043` are compared in the LLVM
  lane proper and pass (`041` is a reject probe: skipped). Found, same family, NOT fixed
  (pre-existing; gap rows at merge): an EXPLICIT compute as a module-level call argument,
  `f(m1 |> compute, 2)` ('m1' was not declared; the bare `f(m1)` is forced and runs); a
  deferred TUPLE as a tuple member, `(p, 1.0)` with `p = (m1, m2)` ('p' was not declared); a
  tuple of computes inside an `if` ('m1' was not declared); `let g2 = g` over a deferred map
  prints `g` in the C++ lane and nothing in the interpreter; and a PLAIN tuple of two failing
  scalar members with no compute member, `(a((a(0) : Row)), 1 / z(0))`, reports the division
  (BL8013) in the C++ lane -- make_tuple's right-to-left argument order -- and the cast (BL8006)
  in the LLVM lane.
